# Research: subset-b-000578

Grouped research for ceph-client Linux IIO Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering ADC, ADDAC, AFE, amplifier, CDC, chemical sensor, DAC, filter, and frequency bindings. Each section preserves the source path and is wrapped for deterministic reconciliation into source-tree-aligned per-file reports.

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/st,stm32-adc.yaml -->
# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/st,stm32-adc.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/st,stm32-adc.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `STMicroelectronics STM32 ADC`, a IIO analog-to-digital converter binding. STM32 ADC is a successive approximation analog-to-digital converter. It has several multiplexed input channels. Conversions can be performed in single, continuous, scan or discontinuous mode. Result of the ADC is stored in a left-aligned or right-aligned 32-bit data register. Conversions can be launched in software or using hardware triggers. The analog watchdog feature allows the application to detect if the input voltage goes beyond the user-defined, higher or lower thresholds. Each STM32 ADC block can have up to 3 ADC instances. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 4 compatible tokens: `st,stm32f4-adc-core`, `st,stm32h7-adc-core`, `st,stm32mp1-adc-core`, `st,stm32mp13-adc-core`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `st,max-clk-rate-hz`, `vdda-supply`, `vref-supply`, `booster-supply`, `vdd-supply`, `st,syscfg`, `interrupt-controller`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `access-controllers`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `vdda-supply`, `vref-supply`, `interrupt-controller`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; required properties found across nested schemas include `#address-cells`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#io-channel-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupt-controller`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `vdda-supply`, `vref-supply`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `interrupts` routes data-ready, error, or controller IRQs; `clocks` binds named clock provider inputs; `clock-names` binds named clock provider inputs; `vdda-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `vref-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `booster-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `vdd-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `#address-cells` enables validated child channel nodes; `#size-cells` enables validated child channel nodes. Patterned child schemas are `^adc@[0-9]+$`; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Fabrice Gasnier <fabrice.gasnier@foss.st.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/adc/stm32-adc-core.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32f429.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32h743.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32mp131.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32mp133.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32mp151.dtsi`. The file includes 3 example blocks for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this file contains 16 top-level properties and 28 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/st,stm32-adc.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/st,stm32-adc.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/st,stm32-dfsdm-adc.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/st,stm32-dfsdm-adc.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `STMicroelectronics STM32 DFSDM ADC device driver`, a IIO analog-to-digital converter binding. STM32 DFSDM ADC is a sigma delta analog-to-digital converter dedicated to interface external sigma delta modulators to STM32 micro controllers. It is mainly targeted for: - Sigma delta modulators (motor control, metering...) - PDM microphones (audio digital microphone) It features up to 8 serial digital interfaces (SPI or Manchester) and up to 4 filters on stm32h7 or 6 filters on stm32mp1. Each child node matches with a filter instance. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; required properties found across nested schemas include `#address-cells`, `#io-channel-cells`, `#size-cells`, `#sound-dai-cells`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `dma-names`, `dmas`, `interrupts`, `io-backends`, `io-channels`, `reg`, `st,adc-channels`, `st,filter-order`. The binding acts as a SPI child device. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `clocks` binds named clock provider inputs; `clock-names` binds named clock provider inputs; `#address-cells` enables validated child channel nodes; `#size-cells` enables validated child channel nodes. Patterned child schemas are `^filter@[0-9]+$`; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. The binding acts as a schema fragment or parent-managed child. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Daniel Baluta <daniel.baluta@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/adc/ti-ads1015.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/at91-natte.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx53-kp-ddc.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6dl-prtmvt.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6dl-prtrvt.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6q-prti6q.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6q-prtwd2.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6qdl-apalis-v1.2.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6qdl-colibri-v1.2.dtsi`, and 4 more. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this file contains 6 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/ti,ads1015.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

The TI ADS1119 is a precision 16-bit ADC over I2C that offers single-ended and differential measurements using a multiplexed input. The ADS131E0x are a family of multichannel, simultaneous sampling, 24-bit, delta-sigma, analog-to-digital converters (ADCs) with a built-in programmable gain amplifier (PGA), internal reference and an onboard oscillator. The communication with ADC chip is via the SPI bus (mode 1). https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/ads131e08.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. The binding acts as a SPI child device. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 2 compatible tokens: `ti,tlc3541`, `ti,tlc4541`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vref-supply`, `#io-channel-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vref-supply`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`, `vref-supply`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `vref-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `#io-channel-cells` connects provider/consumer IIO channels. Patterned child schemas are none; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Phil Reid <preid@electromag.com.au>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/adc/ti-tlc4541.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Because this file contains 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/ti,tlc4541.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/xlnx,zynqmp-ams.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/xlnx,zynqmp-ams.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Xilinx Zynq Ultrascale AMS controller`, a IIO analog-to-digital converter binding. The AMS (Analog Monitoring System) includes an ADC as well as on-chip sensors that can be used to sample external voltages and monitor on-die operating conditions, such as temperature and supply voltage levels. The AMS has two SYSMON blocks which are PL (Programmable Logic) SYSMON and PS (Processing System) SYSMON. All designs should have AMS registers, but PS and PL are optional. The AMS controller can work with only PS, only PL and both PS and PL configurations. Please specify registers according to your design. Devicetree should always have AMS module property. Providing PS & PL module is optional. AMS Channel Details ``````````````````` Sysmon Block |Channel| Details |Measurement |Number | |Type --------... It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 1 compatible token: `xlnx,zynqmp-ams`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `ranges`; required properties found across nested schemas include `clocks`, `compatible`, `ranges`, `reg`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `interrupts` routes data-ready, error, or controller IRQs; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `clocks` binds named clock provider inputs; `#address-cells` enables validated child channel nodes; `#size-cells` enables validated child channel nodes; `#io-channel-cells` connects provider/consumer IIO channels. Patterned child schemas are none; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Salih Erim <salih.erim@amd.com>, Conall O'Griofa <conall.ogriofa@amd.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/adc/xilinx-ams.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/xilinx/zynqmp.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this file contains 10 top-level properties and 11 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/adc/xlnx,zynqmp-ams.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/afe/temperature-sense-rtd.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/afe/temperature-sense-rtd.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Temperature Sense RTD`, a IIO analog front-end/rescale binding. RTDs (Resistance Temperature Detectors) are a kind of temperature sensors used to get a linear voltage to temperature reading within a give range (usually 0 to 100 degrees Celsius). When an io-channel measures the output voltage across an RTD such as a PT1000, the interesting measurement is almost always the corresponding temperature, not the voltage output. This binding describes such a circuit. The general transfer function here is (using SI units) V = R(T) * iexc R(T) = r0 * (1 + alpha * T) T = 1 / (alpha * r0 * iexc) * (V - r0 * iexc) The following circuit matches what's in the examples section. 5V0 ----- | +---+----+ | R 5k | +---+----+ | V 1mA | +---- Vout | +---+----+ | PT1000 | +---+----+ | ----- GND It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/afe/temperature-transducer.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/afe/temperature-transducer.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Temperature Transducer`, a IIO analog front-end/rescale binding. A temperature transducer is a device that converts a thermal quantity into any other physical quantity. This binding applies to temperature to voltage (like the LTC2997), and temperature to current (like the AD590) linear transducers. In both cases these are assumed to be connected to a voltage ADC. When an io-channel measures the output voltage of a temperature analog front end such as a temperature transducer, the interesting measurement is almost always the corresponding temperature, not the voltage output. This binding describes such a circuit. The general transfer function here is (using SI units) V(T) = Rsense * Isense(T) T = (Isense(T) / alpha) + offset T = 1 / (Rsense * alpha) * (V + offset * Rsense... # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/amplifiers/adi,adl8113.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/amplifiers/adi,adl8113.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Analog Devices ADL8113 Low Noise Amplifier with integrated bypass switches`, a IIO amplifier or attenuator binding. The ADL8113 is a 10MHz to 12GHz Low Noise Amplifier with integrated bypass switches controlled by two GPIO pins (VA and VB). The device supports four operation modes: - Internal Amplifier: VA=0, VB=0 - Signal passes through the internal LNA - Internal Bypass: VA=1, VB=1 - Signal bypasses through internal path - External Bypass A: VA=0, VB=1 - Signal routes from RFIN to OUT_A and from IN_A to RFOUT - External Bypass B: VA=1, VB=0 - Signal routes from RFIN to OUT_B and from IN_B to RFOUT https://www.analog.com/en/products/adl8113.html It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `adi,adl8113`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad3530r.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad3530r.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Analog Devices AD3530R and Similar DACs`, a IIO digital-to-analog converter binding. The AD3530/AD3530R (8-channel) and AD3531/AD3531R (4-channel) are low-power, 16-bit, buffered voltage output digital-to-analog converters (DACs) with software-programmable gain controls, providing full-scale output spans of 2.5V or 5V for reference voltages of 2.5V. These devices operate from a single 2.7V to 5.5V supply and are guaranteed monotonic by design. The "R" variants include a 2.5V, 5ppm/°C internal reference, which is disabled by default. Datasheet can be found here: https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ad3530_ad530r.pdf https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ad3531-ad3531r.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vdd-supply`, `iovdd-supply`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `iovdd-supply`, `reg`, `vdd-supply`. The binding acts as a SPI child device. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `vdd-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `iovdd-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `io-channels` connects provider/consumer IIO channels; `ref-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset sequencing; `ldac-gpios` describes control/status GPIO wiring. Patterned child schemas are none; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. Because this file contains 10 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad3530r.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad3552r.yaml -->
# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad3552r.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad3552r.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Analog Devices AD2552R DAC device driver`, a IIO digital-to-analog converter binding. Bindings for the Analog Devices AD3552R DAC device and similar. Datasheet can be found here: https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ad3541r.pdf https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ad3542r.pdf https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ad3551r.pdf https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ad3552r.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 4 compatible tokens: `adi,ad3541r`, `adi,ad3542r`, `adi,ad3551r`, `adi,ad3552r`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`, `reset-gpios`, `ldac-gpios`, `vref-supply`, `adi,vref-out-en`, `adi,sdo-drive-strength`, `io-backends`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`; required properties found across nested schemas include `adi,gain-offset`, `adi,gain-scaling-n`, `adi,gain-scaling-p`, `adi,output-range-microvolt`, `adi,rfb-ohms`, `compatible`, `custom-output-range-config`, `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`. The binding acts as a SPI child device. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset sequencing; `ldac-gpios` describes control/status GPIO wiring; `vref-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `#address-cells` enables validated child channel nodes; `#size-cells` enables validated child channel nodes. Patterned child schemas are `^channel@([0-1])$`; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are `channel@0`, `channel@1`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Nuno Sá <nuno.sa@analog.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/dac/ad3552r-hs.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/dac/ad3552r.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Because this file contains 11 top-level properties and 19 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad3552r.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad3552r.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad5696.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad5696.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Analog Devices AD5696 and similar I2C multi-channel DACs`, a IIO digital-to-analog converter binding. Binding for Analog Devices AD5696 and similar multi-channel DACs It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 14 compatible tokens: `adi,ad5311r`, `adi,ad5337r`, `adi,ad5338r`, `adi,ad5671r`, `adi,ad5675r`, `adi,ad5691r`, `adi,ad5692r`, `adi,ad5693`, `adi,ad5693r`, `adi,ad5694`, `adi,ad5694r`, `adi,ad5695r`, `adi,ad5696`, `adi,ad5696r`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vcc-supply`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `vcc-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies. Patterned child schemas are none; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Michael Auchter <michael.auchter@ni.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/dac/ad5696-i2c.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this file contains 3 top-level properties and 3 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad5696.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad5696.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad5770r.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad5770r.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Analog Devices AD5770R DAC device driver`, a IIO digital-to-analog converter binding. Bindings for the Analog Devices AD5770R current DAC device. Datasheet can be found here: https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/AD5770R.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 1 compatible token: `adi,ad5770r`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `avdd-supply`, `iovdd-supply`, `vref-supply`, `adi,external-resistor`, `reset-gpios`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `channel@0`, `channel@1`, `channel@2`. Top-level required properties are `reg`, `channel@0`, `channel@1`, `channel@2`, `channel@3`, `channel@4`, `channel@5`; required properties found across nested schemas include `channel@0`, `channel@1`, `channel@2`, `channel@3`, `channel@4`, `channel@5`, `reg`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `avdd-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `iovdd-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `vref-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset sequencing; `#address-cells` enables validated child channel nodes; `#size-cells` enables validated child channel nodes. Patterned child schemas are `^channel@([3-5])$`; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are `channel@0`, `channel@1`, `channel@2`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Marcelo Schmitt <marcelo.schmitt@analog.com>, Nuno Sá <nuno.sa@analog.com>. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad8801.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad8801.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Analog Devices AD8801 and AD8803 DACs`, a IIO digital-to-analog converter binding. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 2 compatible tokens: `adi,ad8801`, `adi,ad8803`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vrefh-supply`, `vrefl-supply`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vrefh-supply`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`, `vrefh-supply`, `vrefl-supply`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `vrefh-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `vrefl-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies. Patterned child schemas are none; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Jonathan Cameron <jic23@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Because this file contains 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/adi,ad8801.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

Analog Devices Generic AXI DAC IP core for interfacing a DAC device with a high speed serial (JESD204B/C) or source synchronous parallel interface (LVDS/CMOS). Usually, some other interface type (i.e SPI) is used as a control interface for the actual DAC, while this IP core will interface to the data-lines of the DAC and handle the streaming of data from memory via DMA into the DAC. https://wiki.analog.com/resources/fpga/docs/axi_dac_ip https://analogdevicesinc.github.io/hdl/library/axi_ad3552r/index.html It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Analog Devices LTC2688 16 channel, 16 bit, +-15V DAC https://www.analog.com/media/en/technical-documentation/data-sheets/ltc2688.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 1 compatible token: `adi,ltc2688`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vcc-supply`, `iovcc-supply`, `vref-supply`, `clr-gpios`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `vcc-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `iovcc-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `vref-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `clr-gpios` describes control/status GPIO wiring; `#address-cells` enables validated child channel nodes; `#size-cells` enables validated child channel nodes. Patterned child schemas are `^channel@([0-9]|1[0-5])$`; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

MCP4728 is a quad channel, 12-bit voltage output Digital-to-Analog Converter with non-volatile memory and I2C compatible Serial Interface. https://www.microchip.com/en-us/product/mcp4728 It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `microchip,mcp4728`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vdd-supply`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vdd-supply`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`, `vdd-supply`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `vdd-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies. Patterned child schemas are none; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Andrea Collamati <andrea.collamati@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/dac/mcp4728.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this file contains 3 top-level properties and 3 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/microchip,mcp4728.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/microchip,mcp4728.yaml -->

Supports MCP48x1 (single channel) and MCP48x2 (dual channel) series of DACs. Device supports simplex communication over SPI in Mode 0 and Mode 3. +---------+--------------+-------------+ | Device | Resolution | Channels | |---------|--------------|-------------| | MCP4801 | 8-bit | 1 | | MCP4802 | 8-bit | 2 | | MCP4811 | 10-bit | 1 | | MCP4812 | 10-bit | 2 | | MCP4821 | 12-bit | 1 | | MCP4822 | 12-bit | 2 | +---------+--------------+-------------+ Datasheet: MCP48x1: https://ww1.microchip.com/downloads/en/DeviceDoc/22244B.pdf MCP48x2: https://ww1.microchip.com/downloads/en/DeviceDoc/20002249B.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/rohm,bd79703.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/rohm,bd79703.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `ROHM BD79700, BD79701, BD79702 and BD79703 DACs`, a IIO digital-to-analog converter binding. The ROHM BD7970[0,1,2,3] are 8-bit DACs. The BD79700 has 2 channels, BD79701 3 channels, BD79702 4 channels and BD79703 has 6 channels. Datasheets for BD79702 and BD79703 can be found from https://fscdn.rohm.com/en/products/databook/datasheet/ic/data_converter/dac/bd79702fv-lb_bd79703fv-lb-e.pdf and for the BD79700 and the BD79701 from https://fscdn.rohm.com/en/products/databook/datasheet/ic/data_converter/dac/bd79700fvm-lb_bd79701fvm-lb-e.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 4 compatible tokens: `rohm,bd79700`, `rohm,bd79701`, `rohm,bd79702`, `rohm,bd79703`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`, `vfs-supply`, `vcc-supply`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`, `vcc-supply`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`, `spi-max-frequency`, `vcc-supply`, `vfs-supply`. The binding acts as a SPI child device. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `vfs-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `vcc-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies. Patterned child schemas are none; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Matti Vaittinen <mazziesaccount@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`, `if`, `then`. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/dac/rohm-bd79703.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Because this file contains 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/rohm,bd79703.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/rohm,bd79703.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/st,stm32-dac.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/st,stm32-dac.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `STMicroelectronics STM32 DAC`, a IIO digital-to-analog converter binding. The STM32 DAC is a 12-bit voltage output digital-to-analog converter. The DAC may be configured in 8 or 12-bit mode. It has two output channels, each with its own converter. It has built-in noise and triangle waveform generator and supports external triggers for conversions. The DAC's output buffer allows a high drive output current. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its main job is to make board DTS nodes describe IIO hardware, IIO providers, or IIO consumers with the resources the matching kernel driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema itself: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and the accepted child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 2 compatible tokens: `st,stm32f4-dac-core`, `st,stm32h7-dac-core`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `resets`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `vref-supply`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `access-controllers`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `vref-supply`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; required properties found across nested schemas include `#address-cells`, `#io-channel-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`, `vref-supply`. The binding acts as a bus or MMIO child node selected by parent bus address cells. Important roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the bus chip-select/address or MMIO range; `resets` controls hardware reset sequencing; `clocks` binds named clock provider inputs; `clock-names` binds named clock provider inputs; `vref-supply` links the device to regulator framework supplies; `#address-cells` enables validated child channel nodes; `#size-cells` enables validated child channel nodes. Patterned child schemas are `^dac@[1-2]+$`; named child-channel properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit named channel child nodes. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies the top-level and nested `properties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditionals, checks required fields, validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. At boot there is no YAML execution: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, then IIO, regulator, clock, GPIO, IRQ, SPI/I2C/platform, DMA, and firmware helpers consume the resources named by this contract. For schemas with `channel@N` or patterned child nodes, parent address/size cells control how per-channel configuration is discovered by the driver.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required resource names, channel indices, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts become long-lived contracts for DTS/DTB producers. Runtime state such as ADC samples, DAC output values, buffer enablement, calibration, oversampling, alarms, or data-ready interrupts belongs to the corresponding IIO driver and hardware; this schema only constrains how that stateful device is described.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Fabrice Gasnier <fabrice.gasnier@foss.st.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Driver integration is through the OF `compatible` table, while subsystem integration is through Devicetree common schemas, IIO channel/provider conventions, `io-channels` consumers, bus bindings, regulators, clocks, interrupts, GPIO descriptors, DMA channels, and any child channel schema referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iio/dac/stm32-dac-core.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32f429.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32h743.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32mp151.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift. Changing compatible values or fallback ordering can break driver matching; renaming supplies, clocks, interrupts, GPIOs, or `#io-channel-cells` breaks probe-time resource lookup; tightening required properties or child-node patterns can reject existing boards. Channel-indexed schemas need care because driver channel tables, allowed `reg` values, and analog pin functions must stay aligned. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this file contains 9 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, broad cleanups should be checked against examples, all compatible variants, and in-tree DTS users rather than only the nominal device named in the filename.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/st,stm32-dac.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. High-value negative tests remove required supplies/clocks/IRQs, use bad channel `reg` values, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every conditional compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe with regulators and clocks enabled, correct IIO channel registration, valid `io-channels` consumers, data-ready IRQ behavior where modeled, buffered capture or DAC output where relevant, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/iio/dac/st,stm32-dac.yaml -->
